2.3     Nonlinear forty-nine-path section
Lemma 2.8 (Finite-cell analytic atlas and uniform Cauchy bounds). Let P49 be the finite path atlas
for one retained cell and its finite aligned-reflection template family. There exist fixed radii 0 < η0 < η1 ,
uniform under the standing convention, such that the product exponential, multiplication, inversion,
logarithm, and Karcher-mean maps defining every template extend holomorphically from Tη0 to Tη1 .
   Write the canonically rescaled local block as

                             B(x) = Lx + N (x),       N (0) = 0,             DN (0) = 0.

For every exponential kernel rate 0 < m < mP and r = 2, 3, there is a finite constant

                                             CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ) < ∞

such that
                                    sup ∥Dr N (x)∥Km ≤ CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ),
                                   x∈Tη0

uniformly under the standing convention. The same constants, enlarged by a finite maximum, serve the
aligned-reflection templates.
Proof. For one retained cell the block depends on finitely many g coordinates. The exponential and
multiplication maps are entire, while inversion and the principal logarithm are holomorphic on a fixed
neighborhood of the identity. At the flat field the Karcher critical point has positive Hessian on the
retained tangent space; the analytic implicit-function theorem therefore supplies a unique holomorphic
Karcher mean on a fixed complex neighborhood. The path family and the reflected template family are
finite, so one may choose common nested radii 0 < η0 < η1 for all of them. On the larger finite-dimensional
complex polydisc, every elementary map and their finite compositions are holomorphic and uniformly
bounded. Cauchy’s integral estimate on the smaller polydisc gives uniform derivative bounds through
order three with denominator powers of η1 − η0 .
   The global block is obtained by translating this fixed finite cell. Each output row meets only a fixed
number of input cells, and each input column enters only a fixed number of output cells. Multiplying the
finite-cell Cauchy bounds by these fixed incidence numbers gives the two-sided exponentially weighted
Schur-kernel bounds. These incidence numbers do not depend on the total volume or preceding weak
depth. The aligned-reflection family is finite, so one takes a finite maximum.
Proposition 2.9 (Model-specific nonlinear normal section for the forty-nine-path block). Let

                             pm := ∥P0 ∥Km < 0.546,           C2 := CN,2 (m; η0 , η1 ),

and choose
                                                               1
                                                                    
                                                          η0
                                           ρm = min          ,           .
                                                          4 4pm C2
For every retained coordinate w with ∥w∥Km ≤ ρm , the retained-space equation

                                               y + N (P0 y) = w

has a unique solution y = T (w, 0) in ∥y∥Km ≤ 2ρm . The zero-detail normal section

                                              S(w) := P0 T (w, 0)

satisfies
                                  B(S(w)) = w,        ∥S(w)∥Km ≤ 2pm ρm .
It is analytic and exponentially quasi-local, and its first three marked derivatives have volume- and
depth-independent bounds. The same conclusion holds on the aligned-reflection templates after taking the
minimum radius over that finite family.
Proof. For fixed w, define
                                            Φw (y) := w − N (P0 y).
If ∥y∥ ≤ 2ρm , then ∥P0 y∥ ≤ η0 /2. Since DN (0) = 0, Lemma 2.8 gives
                                                                                 pm  1
                          ∥Dy Φw (y)∥ = ∥DN (P0 y)P0 ∥ ≤ 2p2m C2 ρm ≤               < .
                                                                                  2  2

                                                          9
The second-order integral formula gives
                                         1                            pm
                          ∥N (P0 y)∥ ≤     C2 ∥P0 y∥2 ≤ 2C2 p2m ρ2m ≤    ρm < ρm .
                                         2                             2
Thus Φw maps the 2ρm ball into itself and is a contraction. For its fixed point,

                             B(P0 T ) = LP0 T + N (P0 T ) = T + N (P0 T ) = w.

Analyticity follows from the analytic implicit-function theorem. Differentiation through order three gives
finite Bell polynomials in
                               (I + DN (S)P0 )−1 , P0 , D2 N, D3 N.
The inverse norm is below two and the other factors are bounded by Theorem 2.4 and Lemma 2.8.

3     Charged chart complement and irregular sector
          √
Put g = u and Rg = g −2ε with 0 < ε < 1/6. The symbol χ > 0 denotes the curvature-bad threshold
in this section and in the following two irregular-sector sections; the rooted support exponent remains
denoted by κ elsewhere.
   For an enlarged finite tube cell Q+ define the faithful fine Wilson charge and retained coherent deficit
by the same representation-normalized plaquette function used in the microscopic measure:
                                     X                              X
                        AQ+ (U ) =      wG,ρ (Up ),     DQ (U ) =       wG,ρ (Wp′ ).
                                    p⊂Q+                            p′ ⊂Q′

Let TQ be a fixed smooth invariant dispersion function formed from the squared relative-loop distances of
the forty-nine path inputs. It vanishes on the flat set, its small sublevel set lies inside the common Karcher
chart, and it uses only one fixed enlarged-cell template. On that sublevel set the retained holonomies
Wp′ are defined by the local Karcher atlas. Multiply the displayed retained deficit by a fixed chart cutoff
that is one on a prescribed smaller Karcher sublevel and zero before the edge of the larger Karcher tube;
this gives a smooth invariant extension to the complete finite-cell configuration space. The threshold tch
below is chosen so that the entire curvature-label support lies where this cutoff equals one.
Lemma 3.1 (Representation-normalized charge and derivative ledger). For every fixed G, faithful ρ,
and integer 0 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3, there are finite constants depending only on the fixed cell atlas such that

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

                             cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2       (∥X∥ ≤ r0,G ),
and
             ∥DwG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG wG,ρ (U )1/2 ,       ∥Dr wG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG,r     (2 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3).
The same bounds, with finite template constants, hold after composition with every Wilson word, retained
plaquette word, reflected word, or seam word used below.
Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

Lemma 3.2 (Simultaneous selected-anchor derivative). Select the first fine link of every straight two-link
anchor as the anchor variable; these links are distinct. Hold every remaining fine link in a detail list Z,
and write the simultaneous retained-coordinate map in product exponential charts as
                                                 b Z : a 7−→ w.
                                                 B

On the common weak chart,
                                    b Z (a) = ϑ(I + KZ (a)),            7
                                   DB                              ϑ=     ,
                                                                        8

where KZ has fixed propagation range and
                                                                                  1
                              sup max{∥KZ (a)∥1→1 , ∥KZ (a)∥∞→∞ } ≤                 .
                               a                                                  4
The same row and column estimates hold after the fixed marked derivatives used below.
Proof. At the flat field, the selected straight input contributes ϑI. The cross-anchor cancellation can be
read directly from the flat forty-nine-path symbol. Its diagonal detour part is proportional to
                                                      kµ Y
                                              cos          cos kλ ,
                                                       2
                                                        λ̸=µ

so in position space every detour coefficient has an odd displacement in all three transverse directions
relative to the selected all-even anchor column. For µ ̸= ν, the factor
                                        kν
                           sin kµ sin      (2 cos kα cos kβ + cos kα + cos kβ + 2)
                                        2
has an odd µ displacement. Thus no detour term lands on another selected anchor link; the orientation
signs are the sine signs in this formula. The flat simultaneous derivative is therefore exactly ϑI. Away
from the flat field, every entry is an analytic function of finitely many path holonomies and differs from
its flat value by O(g(1 + Rg )). The forty-nine-path tubes have fixed row and column incidence. Shrinking
the common weak threshold makes the two Schur sums at most 1/4. Chart-trivialization and every fixed
mark add only another finite-incidence O(g(1 + Rg )) term.

Theorem 3.3 (Fixed-chart global anchor coordinate). For fixed details Z, the map B
                                                                                 b Z is bi-Lipschitz
on the convex product chart and a diffeomorphism onto its image. More precisely,
                             3                      b Z (b)∥2 ≤ ϑ 5 ∥a − b∥2 .
                                          b Z (a) − B
                            ϑ ∥a − b∥2 ≤ ∥B
                             4                                    4
Every principal subproblem obtained by fixing the exterior anchors has the same lower Lipschitz bound
and a volume-independent inverse derivative.
Proof. For v = a − b, integrate the derivative along the segment from b to a. Schur’s test gives
∥KZ ∥2→2 ≤ 1/4, proving both bounds. The lower bound gives injectivity; the inverse-function theorem
gives the smooth inverse. A principal submatrix of KZ has no larger row or column sum, so the same proof
applies with exterior anchors fixed. Higher fixed marked derivatives follow by differentiating (I + KZ )−1
and using its Neumann series.
Theorem 3.4 (Finite-cell retention and chart charge). There are constants Cret < ∞, cch > 0, and
tch > 0, depending only on the fixed cell atlas, such that

                                            DQ (U ) ≤ Cret AQ+ (U )

for every fine configuration. Moreover,

                                   TQ (U ) ≥ tch /4     =⇒     AQ+ (U ) ≥ cch .

The constants are independent of total volume, ultraviolet depth, periodic wrapping, and the aligned-
reflection template.
Proof. Use a boundary-rooted tree gauge on the fixed finite contractible cell. By Lemma 3.1, the zero set
of AQ+ is precisely the flat set. On that set all common-endpoint paths agree, hence DQ = TQ = 0. Near
the flat set, the representation-normalized quadratic comparison and the grounded finite-cell Poincaré
inequality give
                                          AQ+ ≍ ∥dX∥2 ≍ ∥X∥2 ,
while smoothness of the finite path atlas and the fact that every retained plaquette word equals the
identity on the flat set give DQ + TQ ≤ C∥X∥2 . This proves the retention inequality on one neighborhood
of the flat set.
   Remove a smaller such neighborhood. On the resulting compact set, AQ+ has a strictly positive
minimum and the smoothly extended DQ is bounded, so their quotient is bounded there as well. This


                                                         11
proves the global displayed inequality without extending a quotient through its zero set. Finally choose
tch inside the chart reserve. The closed set {TQ ≥ tch /4} is disjoint from the flat set; compactness and
Lemma 3.1 give a positive minimum cch of AQ+ on it. Taking the finite maximum and minimum over the
bulk and aligned-reflection templates proves the stated uniformity.

  Choose smooth decreasing cutoffs γD , γ0 , γ1 ∈ [0, 1] with γ0 ≤ γ1 and

                               γD = 1 (DQ ≤ χ2 /4), γD = 0 (DQ ≥ χ2 ),
                               γ0 = 1 (TQ ≤ tch /8), γ0 = 0 (TQ ≥ tch /4),
                               γ1 = 1 (TQ ≤ tch /2), γ1 = 0 (TQ ≥ tch ).

Define the exact labelled partition

               χin                  χcurv

                                                                 χcol              Q = 1 − γ1 .

                                                curv         out


pointwise. The labels are used only inside absolute-value expansions and are recombined before

Lemma 3.5 (Charged support of every irregular label). After fixing 0 < χ ≤ χ0 with χ20 ≤ 4cch , every
curvature, collar, or outer label is supported on

                                                 AQ+ (U ) ≥ c0 χ2

for one fixed c0 > 0. Every derivative of a splice cutoff through order 2sG is supported on the same
charged event and has a fixed-template polynomial bound in χ−1 and the fixed chart thresholds.

Proof. On the support of 1 − γD , either DQ ≥ χ2 /4 or a derivative of γD is present. The retention
inequality gives AQ+ ≥ χ2 /(4Cret ). On the collar and outer supports, or on a derivative of γ0 or γ1 , one
has TQ ≥ tch /8 after decreasing the fixed thresholds once. The compactness argument in Theorem 3.4
gives a fixed positive action charge, which dominates c0 χ2 for χ ≤ χ0 . The derivative bounds through
order 2sG follow from the fixed smooth cutoffs, Lemma 3.1, and the finite cell atlas; differentiation changes
neither transition support nor action charge.

   Fix R0 larger than the diameter of every local variable support in one normalized step. For each
labelled bad block Q, choose a finite integration core IQ containing every variable on which its cutoff and
retention estimate depend, and put
                                             Hb Q = N2R (IQ ).

Lemma 3.7 (Geometric reporting). Let S1 , . . . , Sn be species supports joined by a labelled tree T . Report
each covariance edge by a shortest connector path and each hard-core edge by its fixed bounded collar. If
P is the union of all supports, paths, and finite atlas collars, then
                                           n

for a finite template constant Cgeo .

uniformly bounded number of collar edges for each new attachment. The resulting connected graph spans
P and has the stated number of edges.

and a volume-independent inverse derivative.
Proof. For v = a − b, integrate the derivative along the segment from b to a. Schur’s test gives
∥KZ ∥2→2 ≤ 1/4, proving both bounds. The lower bound gives injectivity; the inverse-function theorem
gives the smooth inverse. A principal submatrix of KZ has no larger row or column sum, so the same proof
applies with exterior anchors fixed. Higher fixed marked derivatives follow by differentiating (I + KZ )−1
and using its Neumann series.
Theorem 3.4 (Finite-cell retention and chart charge). There are constants Cret < ∞, cch > 0, and
tch > 0, depending only on the fixed cell atlas, such that

                                            DQ (U ) ≤ Cret AQ+ (U )

for every fine configuration. Moreover,

                                   TQ (U ) ≥ tch /4     =⇒     AQ+ (U ) ≥ cch .

The constants are independent of total volume, ultraviolet depth, periodic wrapping, and the aligned-
reflection template.
Proof. Use a boundary-rooted tree gauge on the fixed finite contractible cell. By Lemma 3.1, the zero set
of AQ+ is precisely the flat set. On that set all common-endpoint paths agree, hence DQ = TQ = 0. Near
the flat set, the representation-normalized quadratic comparison and the grounded finite-cell Poincaré
inequality give
                                          AQ+ ≍ ∥dX∥2 ≍ ∥X∥2 ,
while smoothness of the finite path atlas and the fact that every retained plaquette word equals the
identity on the flat set give DQ + TQ ≤ C∥X∥2 . This proves the retention inequality on one neighborhood
of the flat set.
   Remove a smaller such neighborhood. On the resulting compact set, AQ+ has a strictly positive
minimum and the smoothly extended DQ is bounded, so their quotient is bounded there as well. This


                                                         11
proves the global displayed inequality without extending a quotient through its zero set. Finally choose
tch inside the chart reserve. The closed set {TQ ≥ tch /4} is disjoint from the flat set; compactness and
Lemma 3.1 give a positive minimum cch of AQ+ on it. Taking the finite maximum and minimum over the
bulk and aligned-reflection templates proves the stated uniformity.

  Choose smooth decreasing cutoffs γD , γ0 , γ1 ∈ [0, 1] with γ0 ≤ γ1 and

                               γD = 1 (DQ ≤ χ2 /4), γD = 0 (DQ ≥ χ2 ),
                               γ0 = 1 (TQ ≤ tch /8), γ0 = 0 (TQ ≥ tch /4),
                               γ1 = 1 (TQ ≤ tch /2), γ1 = 0 (TQ ≥ tch ).

Define the exact labelled partition

                Q = γD γ0 ,
               χin                  χcurv
                                     Q    = (1 − γD )γ0 ,         Q = γ1 − γ0 ,
                                                                 χcol              Q = 1 − γ1 .
                                                                                  χout

Then
                                           Q + χQ
                                          χin        + χcol
                                                        Q + χQ = 1
                                                curv         out


pointwise. The labels are used only inside absolute-value expansions and are recombined before reflection
positivity is invoked.
Lemma 3.5 (Charged support of every irregular label). After fixing 0 < χ ≤ χ0 with χ20 ≤ 4cch , every
curvature, collar, or outer label is supported on

                                                 AQ+ (U ) ≥ c0 χ2

for one fixed c0 > 0. Every derivative of a splice cutoff through order 2sG is supported on the same
charged event and has a fixed-template polynomial bound in χ−1 and the fixed chart thresholds.

Proof. On the support of 1 − γD , either DQ ≥ χ2 /4 or a derivative of γD is present. The retention
inequality gives AQ+ ≥ χ2 /(4Cret ). On the collar and outer supports, or on a derivative of γ0 or γ1 , one
has TQ ≥ tch /8 after decreasing the fixed thresholds once. The compactness argument in Theorem 3.4
gives a fixed positive action charge, which dominates c0 χ2 for χ ≤ χ0 . The derivative bounds through
order 2sG follow from the fixed smooth cutoffs, Lemma 3.1, and the finite cell atlas; differentiation changes
neither transition support nor action charge.

   Fix R0 larger than the diameter of every local variable support in one normalized step. For each
labelled bad block Q, choose a finite integration core IQ containing every variable on which its cutoff and
retention estimate depend, and put
                                             Hb Q = N2R (IQ ).

                                b     meet. A bad seed X is a connected component of this graph, with

                                     |I(X)| + |H(X)| + |H + (X)| ≤ CH |X|,

and the enlarged-cell overlap graph admits a νov -coloring.

depth, wrapping, and reflected template, such that the exact fiber over (W, η) contains an analytic section
sX (W, η) satisfying
                           ∥sX (W, η)∥∞ ≤ Ctr Rg ,      AI(X) (sX ) ≤ Ctr uRg2 |X|.
                                                            √

                                                          16
Proof. Use the model-specific normal coordinates of Proposition 2.9 on every fixed component cell. Start
from the canonical weak prolongation of W : split each coarse logarithm equally between the two straight
fine links, set transverse details to zero, and interpolate across the fixed-width boundary templates to the
actual weak ring datum η. This gives a globally consistent configuration with the displayed pointwise
and action bounds.
   The component-owned anchor problem is a principal subproblem of the simultaneous selected-anchor
map. Theorem 3.3 supplies a uniform lower Lipschitz bound and inverse derivative with the ring anchors
fixed. It therefore gives a unique component anchor correction with constants independent of shape and
topology. Fresh details√are the remaining fiber coordinates. The finite incidence and inverse bounds show
that a ball of radius c u remains inside the common coordinate tube and on the same exact fiber.

Lemma 3.19 (Reference trial plateau). There are fixed d0 < ∞, c, C > 0 such that
                                                   h             2
                                                                         i|X|
                               eb0 qRX (η) GX (η) ≥ cud0 /2 e−C(Rg +1+r)      .

Proof. Integrate only over the plateau ball. Its number of real coordinates is at most d0 |X|, hence its
product volume is bounded below by (cud0 /2 )|X| . On that ball the Wilson action divided by u costs at
most CRg2 |X|. Every fixed Haar, coordinate, coarea, determinant, and anchor-return factor is bounded
above and below on the common finite atlas. The reproduced regular interaction and the cutoff-free
boundary reserve of the exterior-tube theorem cost at most

                                         C(1 + r)|X| + b0 qRX (η).

All good cutoffs equal one by Lemma 3.18. Multiplying the bounds and moving the exterior regulator to
the left gives the result.

support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.

only per-core polynomial/exponential losses, which are absorbed when multiplied by the bad numerator
estimate.

3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′
               sup       eaG d(I(X),I(X )) ∥Qopt,I(X),I(X ′ ) ∥[3] ≤ κG (RG ), κG (RG ) ≤ CG e−mG RG . (1)
                X
                    X ′ ̸=X

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

                                           X               X

For every compatible X the owner map of Definition 3.22 gives the exact identity
                                 X                X

                                      A               X∈X

where each connected term occurs once. The same statement holds for the Haar-coordinate logarithm,
normal-section return factors, local determinant remainders, exponentially reported Schur factors, and
the connected Gaussian normalization below.


                                                      19
Proof. In the exponential matrix algebra, ∥DN P ∥ < η < 1 and the matrix logarithm has its absolutely
convergent word expansion. Report every word on the union of its canonical connector paths. The
permanent support estimate pays all convolutions. Every reported word has one connected support, so
the owner cases are disjoint and exhaustive. The other factors are treated by their Taylor or matrix-log
expansions in the same algebra.

assumed upper/lower and score bounds are paid per vertex and do not alter the cross-link mass. The
connected logarithm removes every empty-support scalar once. This proves the stated uniform row in
exactly the marked norm consumed downstream.

exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′
               sup       eaG d(I(X),I(X )) ∥Qopt,I(X),I(X ′ ) ∥[3] ≤ κG (RG ), κG (RG ) ≤ CG e−mG RG . (1)
                X
                    X ′ ̸=X

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.
Lemma 3.25 (Exact connected coarea allocation). On the permanent weak tube the field-dependent
coarea logarithm has an absolutely convergent connected expansion
                                           X               X
                    − log det(I + DN P ) =     dA ,    sup     eacoa |A| ∥dA ∥[3] < ∞.
                                                             e
                                               A                 A∋e

For every compatible X the owner map of Definition 3.22 gives the exact identity
                                 X                X
                                   dA = Dreg +        DX + Dcross ,
                                      A               X∈X

where each connected term occurs once. The same statement holds for the Haar-coordinate logarithm,
normal-section return factors, local determinant remainders, exponentially reported Schur factors, and
the connected Gaussian normalization below.


                                                      19
Proof. In the exponential matrix algebra, ∥DN P ∥ < η < 1 and the matrix logarithm has its absolutely
convergent word expansion. Report every word on the union of its canonical connector paths. The
permanent support estimate pays all convolutions. Every reported word has one connected support, so
the owner cases are disjoint and exhaustive. The other factors are treated by their Taylor or matrix-log
expansions in the same algebra.

   For the quadratic and exterior-shift part interpolate the off-diagonal precision as Q(t) = Qdiag (I +
        1/2
tK)Qdiag , 0 ≤ t ≤ 1, and apply the finite-dimensional BKAR formula to the block parameters. A
differentiated edge inserts either ⟨yX , KXY yY ⟩ or one of its marked mean/precision variants. No positivity

Corollary 4.2 bounds all Gaussian exponential moments uniformly in the replica matrix and in the number
of cores. The geometric reporting lemma assigns each differentiated tree to its connected core hull. The
exact 1/n! and Lemma 4.3 absorb the vertex factorials. The resulting rooted tree series has link mass at
most 4κ and gives the same denominator as the determinant words.
   A structural or source derivative either differentiates one block of K, one exterior mean, or one one-core
score. Leibniz distributes at most three marks, producing the fourth power in (2). Finally, replacing dγX
by a normalized one-core reference changes only the single-core vertices and normalization scores. Their
assumed upper/lower and score bounds are paid per vertex and do not alter the cross-link mass. The
connected logarithm removes every empty-support scalar once. This proves the stated uniform row in
exactly the marked norm consumed downstream.

remaining terms are finite Bell polynomials in fixed-template derivatives. By Lemma 3.1, the same
statement holds after any |β| ≤ 2sG compact-variable derivatives. Such derivatives add only a fixed
polynomial in u−1 , χ−1 , and |X|; they do not alter the charged support. These polynomial losses multiply
the same action exponential and are absorbed after decreasing its constant c.

absorbed by the assumed action dominance after decreasing c. The finite label sum changes only C.
   The reference density has the same fixed analytic domain without a bad cutoff. Cauchy and the
trial lower bound control its normalized derivatives. Bell-polynomial differentiation of G−1  X preserves
only per-core polynomial/exponential losses, which are absorbed when multiplied by the bad numerator
estimate.

3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′
               sup       eaG d(I(X),I(X )) ∥Qopt,I(X),I(X ′ ) ∥[3] ≤ κG (RG ), κG (RG ) ≤ CG e−mG RG . (1)
                X
                    X ′ ̸=X

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

                                 X                X

                                      A               X∈X

where each connected term occurs once. The same statement holds for the Haar-coordinate logarithm,
normal-section return factors, local determinant remainders, exponentially reported Schur factors, and
the connected Gaussian normalization below.

the owner cases are disjoint and exhaustive. The other factors are treated by their Taylor or matrix-log
expansions in the same algebra.

3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′
               sup       eaG d(I(X),I(X )) ∥Qopt,I(X),I(X ′ ) ∥[3] ≤ κG (RG ), κG (RG ) ≤ CG e−mG RG . (1)
                X
                    X ′ ̸=X

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.
Lemma 3.25 (Exact connected coarea allocation). On the permanent weak tube the field-dependent
coarea logarithm has an absolutely convergent connected expansion
                                           X               X
                    − log det(I + DN P ) =     dA ,    sup     eacoa |A| ∥dA ∥[3] < ∞.
                                                             e
                                               A                 A∋e

For every compatible X the owner map of Definition 3.22 gives the exact identity
                                 X                X
                                   dA = Dreg +        DX + Dcross ,
                                      A               X∈X

where each connected term occurs once. The same statement holds for the Haar-coordinate logarithm,
normal-section return factors, local determinant remainders, exponentially reported Schur factors, and
the connected Gaussian normalization below.


                                                      19
Proof. In the exponential matrix algebra, ∥DN P ∥ < η < 1 and the matrix logarithm has its absolutely
convergent word expansion. Report every word on the union of its canonical connector paths. The
permanent support estimate pays all convolutions. Every reported word has one connected support, so
the owner cases are disjoint and exhaustive. The other factors are treated by their Taylor or matrix-log
expansions in the same algebra.

   For the quadratic and exterior-shift part interpolate the off-diagonal precision as Q(t) = Qdiag (I +
        1/2

assigns a nonnegative quadratic regulator to the two incident vertices. The weighted block row makes

of cores. The geometric reporting lemma assigns each differentiated tree to its connected core hull. The
exact 1/n! and Lemma 4.3 absorb the vertex factorials. The resulting rooted tree series has link mass at
most 4κ and gives the same denominator as the determinant words.
   A structural or source derivative either differentiates one block of K, one exterior mean, or one one-core
score. Leibniz distributes at most three marks, producing the fourth power in (2). Finally, replacing dγX
by a normalized one-core reference changes only the single-core vertices and normalization scores. Their
assumed upper/lower and score bounds are paid per vertex and do not alter the cross-link mass. The
connected logarithm removes every empty-support scalar once. This proves the stated uniform row in
exactly the marked norm consumed downstream.

by a normalized one-core reference changes only the single-core vertices and normalization scores. Their
assumed upper/lower and score bounds are paid per vertex and do not alter the cross-link mass. The
connected logarithm removes every empty-support scalar once. This proves the stated uniform row in
exactly the marked norm consumed downstream.

                                                     21
Lemma 3.29 (History compatibility after exact output formation). At every RG step, form the complete
connected output first, assign its canonical reported hull, and only then compute its regulator allocation
and history certificate. Under this rule:
   (i) every input history has one output owner;
  (ii) no input allocation is copied to two incompatible output histories;
 (iii) concatenation of successive certificates is associative;
 (iv) summing over all certificates recovers the exact unlabelled connected output.
Proof. For one step, canonical collect is a partition of the finite set of labelled connected histories by the
least rooted hull in the fixed ordering. Thus there is one owner and the unlabelled sum is unchanged.
The output allocation is a function of the already collected output gradient and complex Hessian, not of
an input branch. Induct on the number of steps. At the next step the complete preceding certificate
is treated as one input label, so a new canonical hull is appended once. This proves associativity and
excludes copying. Forgetting the labels at every stage gives the exact iterated conditional logarithm by
Fubini and the connected exponential formula.
  Let ζY (u, R) be the complete regular insertion family and define
                    X                                               X
    η0 (u, R) = sup     ea|Y | ∥ζY (u, R)∥[3] , η1 (u, R) = sup sup   ea|Y | ∥DR ζY (u, R)[H]∥[3] .
                  e                                                   ∥H∥[3] ≤1    e
                      Y ∋e                                                             Y ∋e

                              η0 (u, R) ≤ C(uω + ∥R∥[3] ),                 η1 (u, R) ≤ C.

   For the quadratic and exterior-shift part interpolate the off-diagonal precision as Q(t) = Qdiag (I +
        1/2
tK)Qdiag , 0 ≤ t ≤ 1, and apply the finite-dimensional BKAR formula to the block parameters. A
differentiated edge inserts either ⟨yX , KXY yY ⟩ or one of its marked mean/precision variants. No positivity
of KXY is used: the absolute-value allocation
                                                    1
                               |⟨yX , KXY yY ⟩| ≤     ∥KXY ∥(∥yX ∥2 + ∥yY ∥2 )
                                                    2
assigns a nonnegative quadratic regulator to the two incident vertices. The weighted block row makes
the sum of those regulator matrices at every replica vertex at most Cκ. After enlarging RG so 4Cκ < 1,
Corollary 4.2 bounds all Gaussian exponential moments uniformly in the replica matrix and in the number
of cores. The geometric reporting lemma assigns each differentiated tree to its connected core hull. The
exact 1/n! and Lemma 4.3 absorb the vertex factorials. The resulting rooted tree series has link mass at
most 4κ and gives the same denominator as the determinant words.
   A structural or source derivative either differentiates one block of K, one exterior mean, or one one-core
score. Leibniz distributes at most three marks, producing the fourth power in (2). Finally, replacing dγX
by a normalized one-core reference changes only the single-core vertices and normalization scores. Their
assumed upper/lower and score bounds are paid per vertex and do not alter the cross-link mass. The
connected logarithm removes every empty-support scalar once. This proves the stated uniform row in
exactly the marked norm consumed downstream.

                                                     21
Lemma 3.29 (History compatibility after exact output formation). At every RG step, form the complete
connected output first, assign its canonical reported hull, and only then compute its regulator allocation
and history certificate. Under this rule:
   (i) every input history has one output owner;
  (ii) no input allocation is copied to two incompatible output histories;
 (iii) concatenation of successive certificates is associative;
 (iv) summing over all certificates recovers the exact unlabelled connected output.
Proof. For one step, canonical collect is a partition of the finite set of labelled connected histories by the
least rooted hull in the fixed ordering. Thus there is one owner and the unlabelled sum is unchanged.
The output allocation is a function of the already collected output gradient and complex Hessian, not of
an input branch. Induct on the number of steps. At the next step the complete preceding certificate
is treated as one input label, so a new canonical hull is appended once. This proves associativity and
excludes copying. Forgetting the labels at every stage gives the exact iterated conditional logarithm by
Fubini and the connected exponential formula.
  Let ζY (u, R) be the complete regular insertion family and define
                    X                                               X
    η0 (u, R) = sup     ea|Y | ∥ζY (u, R)∥[3] , η1 (u, R) = sup sup   ea|Y | ∥DR ζY (u, R)[H]∥[3] .
                  e                                                   ∥H∥[3] ≤1    e
                      Y ∋e                                                             Y ∋e

                              η0 (u, R) ≤ C(uω + ∥R∥[3] ),                 η1 (u, R) ≤ C.

Every Gaussian cross word is an additional edge of rooted mass at most CκG (RG ) by (5). The strict
                                |X |
core support reserve absorbs C0 . For a bad parent X, the total inherited-child mass is at most C|X|η0 ;

cP ab < ac < a.
   Dominate each connected bipartite incidence graph by a rooted spanning tree rooted at a bad core.
The zero-order marked product estimate has a cubic vertex polynomial and gives (1 − m∗ )−4 . A Frechet
derivative marks one inherited vertex; together with three structural marks this gives the first four
derivatives of the geometric recursion and hence (1 − m∗ )−5 . Lemma 3.29 ensures that this tree sum is
over a partition of the exact histories, not a duplicated majorant. Every sum is rooted, so no volume
factor occurs.

The zero-order marked product estimate has a cubic vertex polynomial and gives (1 − m∗ )−4 . A Frechet
derivative marks one inherited vertex; together with three structural marks this gives the first four
derivatives of the geometric recursion and hence (1 − m∗ )−5 . Lemma 3.29 ensures that this tree sum is
over a partition of the exact histories, not a duplicated majorant. Every sum is rooted, so no volume
factor occurs.

   A structural or source derivative either differentiates one block of K, one exterior mean, or one one-core
score. Leibniz distributes at most three marks, producing the fourth power in (2). Finally, replacing dγX
by a normalized one-core reference changes only the single-core vertices and normalization scores. Their
assumed upper/lower and score bounds are paid per vertex and do not alter the cross-link mass. The
connected logarithm removes every empty-support scalar once. This proves the stated uniform row in
exactly the marked norm consumed downstream.

rows therefore yields the same rooted row for Q−1/2 and for K = Qdiag Qcross Qdiag . This proves (i).

these cumulants through order three without a volume factor. This proves (iii) in the declared rooted
marked norm.

                                                     21
Lemma 3.29 (History compatibility after exact output formation). At every RG step, form the complete
connected output first, assign its canonical reported hull, and only then compute its regulator allocation
and history certificate. Under this rule:
   (i) every input history has one output owner;
  (ii) no input allocation is copied to two incompatible output histories;
 (iii) concatenation of successive certificates is associative;
 (iv) summing over all certificates recovers the exact unlabelled connected output.
Proof. For one step, canonical collect is a partition of the finite set of labelled connected histories by the
least rooted hull in the fixed ordering. Thus there is one owner and the unlabelled sum is unchanged.
The output allocation is a function of the already collected output gradient and complex Hessian, not of
an input branch. Induct on the number of steps. At the next step the complete preceding certificate
is treated as one input label, so a new canonical hull is appended once. This proves associativity and
excludes copying. Forgetting the labels at every stage gives the exact iterated conditional logarithm by
Fubini and the connected exponential formula.
  Let ζY (u, R) be the complete regular insertion family and define
                    X                                               X
    η0 (u, R) = sup     ea|Y | ∥ζY (u, R)∥[3] , η1 (u, R) = sup sup   ea|Y | ∥DR ζY (u, R)[H]∥[3] .
                  e                                                   ∥H∥[3] ≤1    e
                      Y ∋e                                                             Y ∋e

                              η0 (u, R) ≤ C(uω + ∥R∥[3] ),                 η1 (u, R) ≤ C.

Proof. For one step, canonical collect is a partition of the finite set of labelled connected histories by the
least rooted hull in the fixed ordering. Thus there is one owner and the unlabelled sum is unchanged.
The output allocation is a function of the already collected output gradient and complex Hessian, not of
an input branch. Induct on the number of steps. At the next step the complete preceding certificate
is treated as one input label, so a new canonical hull is appended once. This proves associativity and
excludes copying. Forgetting the labels at every stage gives the exact iterated conditional logarithm by
Fubini and the connected exponential formula.

derivatives of the geometric recursion and hence (1 − m∗ )−5 . Lemma 3.29 ensures that this tree sum is
over a partition of the exact histories, not a duplicated majorant. Every sum is rooted, so no volume
factor occurs.

for every auxiliary replica family, and that the parent-normalized rooted tree link constant, after the fixed

shows that the exact 1/n! in the connected logarithm absorbs the product of vertex factorials with only
an exponential loss. The remaining rooted tree sum is the ordinary parent-normalized geometric recursion
in the effective link constant. Fixed structural marks are distributed by Leibniz and change only the
exponential constant depending on N .

4.3    Gaussian reference and optical covariance
This section constructs the Gaussian objects used by the weak block, Wick coordinates, replica forests,
and source connectors. Two objects must not be conflated:
1. the fixed tangent Feynman Gaussian on mean-zero lattice one-forms;
2. its exact conditional detail Gaussian for the linearized forty-nine-path Karcher block.
The first carries the massless retained field. The second is an optical, high-pass covariance with a uniform
gap. The nonlinear Wilson action, Haar and exponential-coordinate density, Karcher coarea factor, cutoffs,
inherited interaction, and source factors are not hidden in either covariance; they are exposed as exact
activities after the normal-fiber change of variables.
4.3.1 Grounded Feynman field and retained torons

Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected

kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a

contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

5.5    Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
                                                      M
                                               J≤D =       J∆ .
                                                         ∆≤D

The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

action-dominance inequality absorbs that polynomial and the trial loss into bu (X). For a one-core reference
there is no Gaussian cross word; the ratio is therefore taken before the remaining product-reference
variables are integrated. Cross-core words remain in the explicit connected row. Summing (HT3) proves
the collar/irregular score row.
   Higher pivot derivatives are obtained by differentiating the normalized-score identity. They are finite
Bell polynomials in derivatives of the complete local action, Jacobian, and normalized cumulants. The
fixed-group atlas and the C ∞ splice templates supply these derivatives through order 2sG ; the plateau
denominator and differentiated total-variation bounds control each normalized cumulant without a
core-volume factor. Thus the hth derivative costs only a fixed polynomial in u−1 .
   Because u ranges in the fixed compact interval I⋆ , all remaining finite powers of u−1 are absorbed into
the displayed CG,h (1 + u−h ) hierarchy. A finite atlas covers the one-pivot compact fiber, so the score
constants are uniform in U , exterior data, volume, and preceding weak depth. Integrating the first score
along a geodesic of length at most diam G proves the oscillation bound. Marking one fixed factor uses
the corresponding marked rows.
   For a finite family of active straight macro-paths, keep every fine link on those active paths in its
original link coordinate. An active path carries one nontrivial retained Peter–Weyl coefficient, or one
bounded scalar probe whose first derivative at the origin is centered. Choose live pivots P = {p1 , . . . , pN }
among the original links of the active paths, with mutually disjoint buffered neighborhoods separated
by distance R and separated from all path endpoints. Condition on every nonpivot fine link, every link
outside the active paths, and all exterior/source data; denote this collection by η. Do not condition on a
retained path product carrying an active coefficient or probe.
   Assign every physical factor by its incidence in the original fine-link support: factors meeting no live
pivot enter H0 , complete owned factors meeting exactly pi enter the ith one-pivot weight, and connected
factors meeting at least two live pivots enter the multipivot remainder. The active retained carriers are
distinguished insertions and are never absorbed into a one-pivot density or counted as physical activities.

pivot derivatives through order 2sG are already included in the three norms in (HT5).

   Lemma 6.28 identifies the trace norm of convolution on L2 (GU ) with the Fourier–Wiener norm and

Definition 6.36 (Actual family incidence trace space). For a family F, let U = U (F). Cut every physical
carrier line at each pivot in U . In every remaining cycle of the physical carrier skeleton choose the least
closure edge and the least selected pivot on that edge; let Rg (F) be the resulting clean pivot set. Then

                   Rg (F) ∩ U = ∅,        every physical carrier cycle meets U ∪ Rg (F).                  (87)

                                    Hg,F := L2 (GU ) ⊗                 L2 (G)p .                          (88)

Lemma 6.37 (Carrier–activity hypergraph coverage and no-reuse allocation). For a connected family
(g, F) form the finite incidence hypergraph Hg,F whose ordinary edges are the physical carrier segments and
whose hyperedge A is incident to the selected pivots IP (A). Root the carrier skeleton by the deterministic
order of Lemma 6.34. Then:
    (i) cutting every carrier at U (F) ∪ Rg (F) leaves an acyclic carrier graph;
   (ii) the depth-first first-incidence rule assigns every activity hyperedge A exactly once, at the least
        incident pivot first reached from the root; its remaining n(A) − 1 incidences are recorded only as
        possible side branches;
  (iii) the three payments are disjoint: the product Fourier–Wiener norm pays the tensor-product nuclear
        cost on U (F), the scalar coverage reserve pays the lost clean-pivot contractions, and the promoted
        magnitude wA,k(A) is inserted exactly once in the rooted spatial recursion.
Consequently no same-edge or cross-edge activity, shared carrier tail, closure edge, or cycle-breaking pivot
is charged twice in (90) or in the recursion (94).
Proof. By definition, every carrier cycle meeting an activity is cut at all of that activity’s incident pivots,
and every remaining activity-free carrier cycle is cut at its least closure pivot in Rg (F). Hence a cycle in
the residual carrier graph would avoid both sets, contradicting (87); this proves (i). On the resulting
rooted forest, deterministic depth-first exploration gives a total order on first visits to pivots. Assign a
hyperedge at the first visited member of its incidence set. This assignment is unique, while every other
incidence can only attach a branch to an already assigned hyperedge, proving (ii). The product-group
convolution of Lemma 6.35 contains the complete activity factor and all covered one-pivot densities before
any scalar split. Lemma 6.31 then separates its nuclear loss from the unused scalar incidence reserve.
The first-incidence exploration retains one and only one copy of the promoted activity magnitude. These
operations act on distinct factors of the majorant, proving (iii) and the final claim.
